#2681. Class 4A second round: Miller, Colonels move past Tomcats: Covington Catholic builds early lead, advances to next round

ASHLAND Kiyan Miller literally carried the load for Covington Catholic Friday night.

The sophomore had 19 carries for 149 rushing yards and three touchdowns during a 57-28 victory at Ashland in the second round of the Class 4A playoffs.

When the Tomcats had a momentous start to the second half, opening with a touchdown and cutting the deficit to two possessions, Miller quashed the rally on his own.

The ensuing Colonels' drive featured five Miller runs, including a 28-yard gain while dragging multiple Ashland defenders downfield.

"It's who he has been all year," Covington Catholic coach Eddie Eviston said. "He's the guy that sets the tone for us. He does a nice job along with those guys up front. They allow us to be very balanced in what we do. We have some weapons on the outside and with our quarterback, but those guys get the much-needed yards"

Compounding the situation, Antonio Rodriguez put the home team behind the sticks on the next drive with a pair of sacks, and the Tomcats had to punt deep in their own territory. Jamir Kirtley collected a short kick on the run and sprinted away from the defense for a 45-yard return to the house.

Ashland could not escape an early 21-point deficit, but coach Chad Tackett hopes his team won't forget the fight and character they showed with their season on the line.

"I am super proud of them," Tackett said. "Our whole thing this week was empty your tank, and they did that Friday night. Our seniors did a phenomenal job leading this football team this year. I was proud of how we played. We played hard. We made mistakes, but we couldn't cut it close enough to get back in it. … It was a tough loss, but I thought the game was a lot closer than the score indicated."

Covington Catholic (8-4) opened the game with a scoring drive. The seven-play possession ended with Miller's first trip to the end zone on a 9-yard run.

Ashland followed with a three-and-out, assisted by a Jonny Lind sack. The Colonels wasted no time attacking with the resulting good field position. Quarterback Dylan Courtney hit Brandon Smith in stride down the middle of the field for a 45-yard touchdown pass.

"Our coaches did a great job of getting our guys prepared on both sides of the ball," Eviston said. "We knew they were explosive with their offense. Our defense did a nice job of getting a couple of negative plays early and put them in a third-and-long situation. It really helped build that lead."

Covington Catholic needed just one snap to score again before the opening quarter ended. Owen Pitzer found a running lane to the end zone on a 36-yard screen pass. Kirtley added to the quick-strike barrage with a short TD run in the first half. Miller supplied another touchdown to give the Colonels a 22-point lead at halftime.

"We knew he was a good back," Tackett said of Miller, "but we were really concerned about their quarterback and tight end. Those two guys on film were outstanding. Cov Cath's four losses are to Beechwood, Highlands, Indianapolis Cathedral and Elder, one of the top teams in their division in Ohio. And they competed in all of those games. We knew they were a good team. We just hoped that we could slow them down enough.

"We started clicking on offense," he added. "We found out we could run the ball and kept them off-balance with our passing game. I thought we moved the ball better than anybody outside of those four teams."

Justin Hughes caught five balls for 108 yards. He collected two TD catches, matching the Tomcats' single-season mark.

Tre Taylor had 48 rushing yards. Mav Ashby saw more carries late in the season after battling injuries for two years. He led Ashland (10-2) in rushing in his final game. Ashby tallied 62 ground yards and scored twice.

"He's coming back from the knee injury, and then he had an ankle injury," Tackett said. "We were using him exclusively on defense. The other guys got rolling, and we tried to get him back in. He's a great back, and his best football is ahead of him. He's going to mid-enroll at Georgetown and he will be an outstanding player for them."

Courtney finished 6 of 8 through the air with 151 passing yards for the Colonels. Mac Cogswell added a late 43-yard touchdown catch.

"We've always built our offense around getting multiple people touches," Eviston said. "The guys that got touches tonight did great things with the ball. It's good to see. It was a fun night offensively."

Covington Catholic's playoff path doesn't get easier, as they travel to Boyle County next week for the state quarterfinal. A convincing road win gives them added momentum.

"It's one thing we talked about," Eviston said. "We would probably be on the road most of the time. We talked about that mentality. It's something that we can't control. Wherever we go, we will kick the ball off and line up. Our guys did a great job of handling that mentality."

#2679. Ground stop results in side judge Don Willard serving as referee for two games on Sunday

Friday’s nationwide ground stop won’t keep NFL teams from getting to their road games on Sunday. It will, however, impact the ability of officials to get to where they need to be.

The NFL has announced that officiating assignments have changed for Week 10. And the situation will result in a backup referee serving as the referee in two games on the same day.

“Due to the ongoing nationwide impact to air travel this weekend, the NFL has adjusted its officiating assignments for Week 10 games, reassigning officials to games within driving distance of their hometowns,” the NFL said in a statement. “The changes will necessitate that three backup referees — umpires Barry Anderson and Duane Heydt and side judge Don Willard [pictured] — will work as referees in the following games: Carolina-New Orleans – Duane Heydt; Jacksonville-Tennessee – Barry Anderson; Miami-Indianapolis and Pittsburgh-Cincinnati – Don Willard.

“Each of the league’s 17 officiating crews have a backup referee identified, and that individual trains throughout the year to work at the referee position should the need arise.”

The situation creates the likely unprecedented situation of a referee working two games in one day. Willard will handle Dolphins-Colts at 1:00 p.m. ET, and then he’ll make the 109-mile trip from Indianapolis to Cincinnati for the Sunday night game.

Making the unprecedented even more unusual is the fact that Willard’s usual crew (led by referee Scott Novak) wasn’t scheduled to work at all this weekend.

The announcement doesn’t address where the referees for those three games will be working, or what their roles will be. Shawn Smith was scheduled to be the referee for Panthers-Saints, Alex Moore was set to be the referee for Jaguars-Titans, Carl Cheffers was due to be the referee for Dolphins-Colts, and Clete Blakeman was slated to be the referee for Steelers-Bengals.

The league’s statement also doesn’t specify other shifts that were required as to the established crews that work together throughout the regular season.

37 points on 15-of-20 shooting, 9 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 block

You're just not winning a game like this when Miami's front court combines for 74 points.

The Miami Heat were in the 98th percentile for offensive rating this tgame (137.1).

Miami dominated inside. They retrieved 34.8% of their missed shots (73rd percentile), had a free-throw rate of 33 (85th percentile), and scored 56 points in the paint. Controlling the paint and forcing your way to the free-throw line is still the most effective way to generate a great offense.

As easy as it would be to blame the Cavs for these issues, sometimes you just have to tip your cap to the other team. Miami had the personnel to take advantage of Cleveland's injuries, and they did.

Zach Lowe made a good point on his most recent pod that I believe is worth expounding on: The Cavs have slowly been evolving their identity.

Cleveland found out in their 2025 series with the Indiana Pacers that they need more well-rounded players who can pass, dribble, and shoot.

Their initial effort in identifying those supporting pieces didn't necessarily work out (Lonzo Ball), but their recent attempts have been much more encouraging. Guys like Peyton Watson, Mario Hezonja, Meleek Thomas, and Tyrese Proctor can fill multiple roles on the court and are more than just specialists. Jaylon Tyson also fits that box, although he was drafted before the Pacers series. This archetype stands in stark contrast with previous supporting players like Dean Wade, Georges Niang, De'Andre Hunter, and Keon Ellis, who were more specialized in one particular skill.

Quietly, the Cavs have shifted from a team with specialists around their core players to one where their core is insulated with more well-rounded skill sets.

It made sense to put specialists around the Cavs in 2023. That team desperately needed more shooting around the original core four after Mitchell's first season in Cleveland. It's nearly impossible to win playoff series if you can't spread the floor in any meaningful way. Prioritizing shooting allowed them to advance in the playoffs in subsequent years, but if they intend to take the next step, they needed to diversify their rotation players.

Early injuries to Sam Merrill and Nae'Qwan Tomlin have given some of the newer supporting players more room to shine, and they've taken advantage of it. Against the New York Knicks, it was Thomas and Proctor who carried them through some of those bench lineups, and it was Watson who made the game-saving block. Here, it was Proctor who provided consistent scoring, Thomas (12 points) who supplied some shot-making at the end of the third to get them back into the game, and Tyson (16 points) who filled in well as a starter.

The Cavs are still figuring some things out, and are far from a finished product. But if you wanted something positive to hang your hat on early in the season, it's the fact that the supporting pieces have the potential to change how this team plays.

Stars still matter. The Cavs will have a tough time winning playoff series this spring if Mitchell or Harden lay an egg. But the emergence of some of these supporting pieces has the potential to change what is asked of the stars during close postseason games. Maybe Thomas can carry some of the scoring load for stretches in the second and third quarters. Maybe Watson's ability to recover defensively alters how the two-big front line operates defensively and further insulates the guards. Maybe Proctor can help bring the ball up against the press, alleviating that burden from the stars.

The Cavs didn't get the win on Friday. Trying to defend a Giannis-led team without Mobley is a tall task. Results notwithstanding, we've seen some encouraging steps forward from some of the supporting cast the last week or so. That could make a huge difference if the stars can get healthy and more in sync than they have been.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2677. Recap: Wake Forest Decimates SMU 49-7

The Deacs have done it AGAIN. For a 5th straight game, Wake Forest has completely dominated their opponent, beating SMU on their home field by 42 points.

Stop me if you've heard this one before, but the Deacs basically won another game in the 1st quarter. SMU won the toss and elected to receive the ball, which turned out to be a costly error. The Wake Forest defense forced a 3 and out, and the Deacs quickly went up 7-0 in about 2 minutes on a 27-yard touchdown run by Ty Clark.

For a 4th straight week, a 1st quarter touchdown to take a 7-0 lead was enough to not lose the game. From that point on, it was just all Deacs—everything went Wake's way in the 1st half. The Deacs stopped SMU on a 4th and 1 on their own 26 yard line and quickly converted that into 7 points. Gio Lopez hit his favorite target Carlos Hernandez for a big 44 yard touchdown pass. Wake recovered an SMU fumble on the SMU 21 yard line—Lopez ran that into the endzone 5 players later. The Deacs even recovered the rare 44-yard onside kick when the Mustangs failed to field a kickoff. By the end of the first half, Wake was up 42-0 and had outgained SMU 248 to 120. That is just complete domination.

To emphasize just how dominant this Wake Forest team is, over their last 4 games, Wake is outscoring their opponents 145-0 in the first half. The Deacs were the first team since 2013 Alabama to score 20+ points in the first half and give up 0 points over a 4 game stretch.

The game was so out of hand, it was even booted from ESPN to ESPN News. I have a hard time believing that would every happen to SEC game, but the Deacs were winning by so many points it was really hard to be mad about anything.

I said all week this was a massive game with ACC Championship implications, and the Deacs showed up in the best way. It has been so enjoyable to continually watch Wake blow the doors off teams in the first half, and then relax and watch them coast to a win in the 2nd half.

Gio Lopez was maybe a step slow with that brace on his leg, but he looked really good in his return after sitting out last week—Lopez finished with 201 passing yards and 3 total touchdowns.

The Deacs completely dominated the line of scrimmage as well, outgaining the Mustangs 286 to 66 yards on the ground—Wake had more rushing yards than SMU had total yards (275). SMU's defense had no answer for the Wake offense, which punted just twice in the whole game. The Mustangs were so desperate for way to keep the Deacs out of the endzone, they even tried having the mascot poop on the goal line just before the 4th quarter. Even that didn't work, as Steele Pizzella, who entered the game in the 3rd quarter, scored on a 25-yard run right after the "accident." Thankfully, he was nowhere near where the incident took place.

Give a ton of credit to the Wake Forest defense, too—the Deacs completely disrupted an SMU offense that was putting up 37 points and 475 yards per game. SMU finally scored a touchdown in the 3rd quarter after a Jack Foley fumble, but Wake shut them out for 2 and half quarters. That included a goal line stand right at the end of the first half to block any chance the Mustangs had of gaining some momentum right before the half. Wake held Kevin Jennings, who entered the game averaging 307 passing yards per game, to 209 yards and picked him off 1 time. Hopefully Wake will lock down DC Scottie Hazelton the same way they locked down Dickert and Ezell in the coming weeks, because this defense has been playing phenomenally all season long.

With Duke, SMU, and Clemson all losing today, the only 2-loss ACC team remaining is Louisville, who the Deacs have already beaten. That means Wake Forest can clinch an ACC Championship Game berth with a win over Georgia Tech next week or Duke in 2 weeks. The Deacs have a chance to turn what has been an already incredible season into something truly historic. Wake should move up into the playoff field after thumping SMU on the road, but with the way the committee views the ACC, I won't hold my breath.

#2676. Miami Dolphins offensive line review: Tracking the guards in Week 9

When you lose 19-9 in a game where your offense only crosses midfield a handful of times, it's usually best to burn the tape and move on.

That may be especially true for the offensive line, where rookie left guard Kadyn Proctor and second-year right guard Jonah Savaiinaea remain key pieces of the Dolphins' future. While the offense struggled as a whole, player development doesn't stop because of one ugly afternoon, and that's especially true for two young guards expected to be part of the foundation moving forward. Each week, we take a closer look at Proctor and Savaiinaea and examine what analysts are saying about their most recent performances.

Listen, it was not a good week for the offensive line – if you were hoping for a bounce-back effort after a rough outing against the Patriots, the tape from the Detroit Lions game offered very little comfort. While much of the post-game attention has been directed toward Aaron Brewer and Austin Jackson, the entire unit struggled against a physical Detroit front. Alain Poupart over at Sports Illustrated called the offensive line the team's most disappointing position group based on PFF rankings (I know, I know):

Brewer is ranked 17th out of 36 qualifying centers.

Jackson is ranked 71st out of 71 tackles. That's not great, Bob.

Jonah Savaiinaea is 69th among 75 guards.

For Kadyn Proctor, while it was undeniably a rough afternoon against the Lions, there is still plenty of reason for long-term optimism. His individual tape showed some correctable rookie mistakes, such as getting too flat on a second-quarter power play and completely missing his linebacker assignment. However, he also flashed his high-end potential later in the game by quickly recognizing a slanting defensive tackle on a trap concept and stopping on a dime to seal the block. While there were instances of missing second-level blocks, Proctor still played with physicality and had some highlight blocks. He continues to flash the talent that made him a first-round pick, and his mistakes largely look like growing pains.

The conversation around Jonah Savaiinaea, however, is becoming a bit more concerning. The Lions frequently isolated him in space by putting five men on the line of scrimmage, and Jonah struggled mightily, particularly when giving up inside moves. On one notable power play, indecision caused Jonah to completely pass up his assigned defender, blowing up the timing of the run. After a solid first three weeks of the season, his recent tape has looked a lot like his 2025 rookie struggles, when he ranked last among all guards. While it is still too early to make a definitive call on his career trajectory, it's fair to ask if he may settle in as a below-average starter rather than the long-term answer we hoped for. Maybe we'll see more of that early-season tape against the Colts.

The tracker will be updated each week throughout the season. For a more in-depth breakdown of Proctor's and Savaiinaea's performance, check out offensive line Coach Eric Smith's film analysis of the game, which you can find on his YouTube channel, Focused on Fins.

#2675. Report: Vikings first reached out to Raiders about Kirk Cousins on "midday Monday"

As it turns out, the Vikings contacted the Raiders about a possible trade for quarterback Kirk Cousins before Monday night’s meltdown from quarterback Carson Wentz.

Via ESPNFL, the Vikings initially contacted the Raiders during “midday Monday,” prior to Minnesota’s Week 9 game against the Bills. The Vikings told the Raiders they wanted to see how things went during the Monday night game before having any further conversations.

Then came Wentz’s 8.9-passer rating performance in a 23-6 loss, and Minnesota mobilized. The Vikings, per the report, contacted the Raiders before 6:00 a.m. PT on Tuesday and commenced the talks that culminated in a deal being done.

Said Raiders G.M. John Spytek during a Tuesday press conference: “I never made one call on Kirk Cousins. And I got a few phone calls. But one real one this morning.”

The Vikings initially offered a fifth-round pick for Cousins, per the report. The report doesn’t specify the Raiders’ opening position.

Once the Vikings moved to a third-round pick, the Raiders pounced.

Beyond avoiding the $10 million guaranteed bonus and clearing the path for rookie No. 1 overall pick Fernando Mendoza (which are mentioned in the article), the Raiders ultimately bought a third-round pick for $650,000 (which is not).

That’s the most attractive aspect of the entire transaction, in our view. Nearly 10 years ago, the Browns bought a second-round pick from the Texans by assuming quarterback Brock Osweiler’s $16 million full guarantee for the 2017 season. Any team would sprint for the chance to purchase a third-round pick in the 2027 draft for only $650,000.

In this case, that bargain came with nine starts from Cousins, four wins, and months of excellent mentorship for Mendoza.

As to the lingering sense in some circles that the Raiders could have gotten even more for Cousins, they risked driving a bargain so hard that the Vikings would have walked away and made a trade with another team.

So it was a win for the Vikings, who got their first choice. It was a win for the Raiders. And it definitely was a win for Cousins, who went from waiting to be benched for Mendoza to having nine games as the starter for a contender — plus the strong possibility that the Vikings will make the playoffs.

#2669. Day’Ron Sharpe is making another leap in rare Nets bright spot

MEMPHIS, Tenn. — Day'Ron Sharpe is making the jump from backup bruiser to full-time starter and complete player.

And so far, the transition has been going well.

Sharpe has an impressive plus-16.7 net rating swing for Brooklyn entering Saturday's game in Memphis. Essentially meaning they are an aggregate 16.7 points per 100 possessions better — offensively and defensively — with the big man on the floor.

That's the fourth-best rating in the entire league among all qualified centers.

"Yeah, he's embracing the new role, and he's adjusting," Nets coach Jordi Fernández said of Sharpe. "He's adjusting with playing bigger minutes. … It comes with, its a lot. And I think he's doing a great job. He is being very diligent with his routine, his body, taking care of himself, his treatment, what he eats. And right now, you can see that he can perform in longer stints and for more minutes.

Brooklyn Nets center Day'ron Sharpe (20) shoots a layup against the Charlotte Hornets on Wednesday, Nov. 4, 2026, at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y. Corey Sipkin for the NY POST

"So, very proud of him, and I think he's impacting the group also. We're playing well and, at times, very well because of him. And what he brings, like yesterday or two days ago, he had huge winning plays at the rim and verticalities and then his passing, rebounding so forth. So I'm very happy and he's got to keep taking steps, keep working."

Over Sharpe's last six games, he's averaged 9.5 points, seven rebounds and 6.5 assists to just 2.2 turnovers — almost a 3-to-1 assist-to-turnover ratio — as he's unlocked his playmaking in dribble handoffs DHOs with Michael Porter Jr. His four straight games with at least five assists are a Nets franchise record for a center.

Brooklyn Nets center Day'ron Sharpe, left, shoots over New York Knicks center Karl-Anthony Towns at Madison Square Garden on Monday, Nov. 2, 2026. Jason Szenes for NY Post

But more importantly, Sharpe — always a powerful rebounder in shorter stints — has provided rim protection, with his career-high tying four blocks in New Orleans giving him 10 over his last five games.

"Like I said before the season started, I feel like I'm trying to be one of the best when it comes to the small details of the game. Extra plays, saving plays, it's all about the position game to me. "Nolan just practiced with the group; see how he feels after," said Fernández. "And then Mikel hasn't practiced, he's not here. See how he feels (Saturday)."

Point guard Ben Saraf was not called up. He's still on G-League assignment along with Joshua Jefferson, Tyler Bilodeau and Grant Nelson.

#2665. Warriors get demolished by Stephon Castle, Spurs

Friday was always going to be an incredibly difficult game for the Golden State Warriors. They were on the road, taking on a San Antonio Spurs team that had won 10 consecutive games. Just on strength of opponent, it was the hardest game of the year for the Dubs.

And then "hard" turned into "impossible" when Steph Curry was injured during Wednesday's loss to the Houston Rockets. With Curry sidelined, the Warriors were, essentially, without hope in this game.

Still, about once a year the Warriors — like most teams — are good for a shocking upset bid when undermanned. Could this be the night where they're competitive against all odds?

It only took a few possessions for the answer to be an emphatic "no." Steve Kerr opted for a small starting lineup — De'Anthony Melton, Brandin Podziemski, Will Richard, Yaxel Lendeborg, and Draymond Green — hoping to outrun a team that they would be catastrophically undersized against, regardless of who they started.

And boy howdy, that — like everything the team tried — did not work. The opening possessions went exactly the way you would expect: the Warriors could not find an inch of space, while the Spurs broke them down. It felt like a team of seven-footers was playing a team of five-footers … it seemed like every Warriors pass was deflected, and every shot was blocked or contested. In the time it took you to figure out how to get this Prime Video game on your TV, San Antonio had taken a 7-0 lead.

They were getting everything they wanted on offense, using the gravity and playmaking of Victor Wembanyama to fully break down a helpless Warriors defense. Kerr called his first timeout less than three minutes into the game, with the Spurs leading 12-0. That timeout helped Golden State's defense a tiny bit, but it did nothing for their offense. 12-0 became 14-0 became 16-0 became 18-0, the best start to a game in San Antonio's history.

Finally, more than halfway through the quarter, the Warriors — who started the game 0-for-11 from the field — got on the board, when Gui Santos absorbed some contact for a tough and-one.

But even that play was emblematic of the struggles, as Santos missed the free throw, and just a few seconds later, the Spurs converted it into a three.

Santos' bucket did not break any dam. Offense was still borderline impossible for the Dubs to come by, though they finally found a few buckets when Wembanyama took a rest. San Antonio's offense, on the other hand, didn't change at all with their superstar sidelined, as Stephon Castle took it upon him to thoroughly feast on Warriors defenders. That, combined with the Spurs making it rain from deep, led to a 38-14 San Antonio advantage after the first quarter. With no Curry, it was clear that the game was already over.

Golden State played much better defense in the second quarter, and the Spurs' offense got a little discombobulated. But that did nothing for the Warriors' offense, which was still stuck on life support. Every bucket was hard to come by — though LJ Cryer had some nice bursts to give fans something to cheer for — and Golden State could do nothing to cut into the deficit. A bad foul on a De'Aaron Fox three with less than a second remaining punctuated an awful half, and the Spurs went to the locker rooms leading 66-33.

The Warriors opened the third quarter with a pair of threes, and then, for all intents and purposes, they walked off the court and called it a night. San Antonio was taller, longer, stronger, more athletic, faster, and happy to flex all of those advantages in every area of the game. The second half was window dressing, and the Spurs weren't even incentivized to take their foot off the gas, since it was an NBA Cup game, and thus, point differential mattered. The lead ballooned to 43 points for the Spurs, before coming back to an ever-so-slightly less-disrespectful 38 by the final buzzer, with San Antonio winning 123-85.

Cryer was the Warriors' lone bright spot, as he scored a career-high 20 points with six assists (and no turnovers!), while shooting 6-for-11 from three-point range. Charles Bassey, who had 11 points and five rebounds, was the only other Golden State player in double figures.

With Curry out, the Dubs are going to rely on Lendeborg and Podziemski to carry the offense, but neither could do so against the ranginess of San Antonio's excellent defense. Lendeborg had far and away the worst game of his young career, as he shot 1-for-13 from the field for three points, seven rebounds, one assist, and three turnovers. Podz wasn't much better, as he had eight points on 2-for-10 shooting. As a team, the Warriors shot a paltry 33.3% (31-for-93) from the field. The Spurs' length gave the Dubs fits, as they shot significantly worse inside the arc than beyond it … they shot 15-for-41 (36.6%) on threes, but just 16-for-52 (30.8%) on twos, while only earning 11 free throws.

Castle led San Antonio with a brilliant game, finishing with 32 points, five rebounds, and six assists. Wembanyama only played 21 minutes, but that was enough for him to have 12 points, 12 rebounds, six assists, one steal, and two blocks.

The Dubs now get the weekend off, before facing the New Orleans Pelicans on Monday.

#2663. Grading quarterback Jared Curtis after Vanderbilt football loss to Alabama

Vanderbilt football lost to Alabama 21-6 at FirstBank Stadium on Nov. 14, failing to score a touchdown for the first time this season.

The Commodores (2-6, 1-6 SEC) played well defensively, but the offense had only 39 rushing yards (not including sacks) and the team committed 14 penalties for 92 yards. Freshman quarterback Jared Curtis had to produce most of the offensive yards himself.

All six points came on a pair of 50-plus yard field goals from Brock Taylor.

Curtis was 25-for-40 passing for 223 yards. He didn't throw any touchdowns or commit any turnovers.

He was sacked five times for 27 yards. He ran four times for 9 yards.

Curtis had an uneven performance against Alabama. On one hand, he didn't commit any turnovers, something that hurt him against Kentucky and Arkansas. However, his efficiency wasn't what it was in some of the earlier games.

He made only one throw of more than 20 yards, missed some open receivers and sometimes completed throws that didn't gain much yardage. He also threw out of the end zone on fourth-and-4, down two touchdowns, when any attempt to make a play would've been better than throwing it away.

Curtis didn't lose Vanderbilt the game − the bigger issues were penalties and an inability to run the ball − but he didn't do anything to win it, either.

#2661. Packers, Broncos explored possible Tony Pollard trade

One week before the trade deadline, teams had interest in Titans running back Tony Pollard. But Tennessee was not yet talking.

The Titans eventually engaged, but no one met their price.

Via ESPNFL, the Packers and Broncos “checked in” on Pollard. The Titans wouldn’t move Pollard for less than a third-round pick.

Both teams had been linked to Colts running back Jonathan Taylor. The Colts, after creating the impression that Taylor would be traded, opted to take the best offer they could get.

The fact that Green Bay and Denver explored acquiring two different tailbacks isn’t much of a vote of confidence in their current depth charts. As to the Packers, it underscores the potentially tenuous status of Josh Jacobs.

Pollard, a fourth-round pick of the Cowboys in 2019, started his career as the backup/understudy to Ezekiel Elliott. Pollard had a breakthrough season in 2022, rushing for 1,007 yards and making it to the Pro Bowl despite Elliott still being the starter. (Elliott gained 876 yards that season.)

Pollard became the full-time starter in 2023, a franchise-tag season. He signed with the Titans in 2024.

He has four straight 1,000-yard seasons. Through eight games in 2026, he has 595 yards. Which puts him on pace for what would be a career-high 1,190 yards.

Pollard is in the final year of a three-year, $21.75 million contract. He’s due to be a free agent in March.

#2660. Giannis, Bam join LeBron, Wade in exclusive Miami Heat club

The Miami Heat know a thing or two about dynamic duos.

Giannis Antetokounmpo and Bam Adebayo each scored 37 points in a 133-121 win over the Cleveland Cavaliers on Friday, Nov. 13. Antetokounmpo added nine rebounds and Adebayo notched 14 boards for a double-double in the NBA Cup matchup at the Kaseya Center.

According to the NBA, the two stars join LeBron James and Dwyane Wade as the only other duo in franchise history to score more than 35 points each in the same game.

James and Wade did so twice after James joined Wade on the Heat via "The Decision" in 2010. On March 14, 2012, Wade scored 36 points and James added 35 in a loss to the Chicago Bulls. They also reached the mark on Feb. 26, 2013 when James had 40 points and Wade tossed in 39 in a double-overtime win over the Sacramento Kings. The two won back-to-back championships in 2012 and 2013.

On Friday, Davion Mitchell added 22 points and 17 assists for Miami in the winning effort. Mitchell's 17 assists matched his career high.

Donovan Mitchell led Cleveland with 25 points, seven assists and four rebounds.

This is Antetokounmpo's first season in Miami after a blockbuster trade with the Milwaukee Bucks that included Tyler Herro, Kel'el Ware, Jaime Jaquez Jr. and Kasparas Jakučionis. The No. 15 overall pick in the 2013 NBA Draft spent his entire career up to this point with the Bucks and decided he wanted a fresh start. He won two league MVPs, was named an All-Star 10 times and won the 2021 NBA Finals in Milwaukee, when he was named Finals MVP.

He will face his former team for the first time on Nov. 18 when the Heat host "A Night in Vice City" to promote the highly-anticipated "Grand Theft Auto 6" video game.

Antetokounmpo is averaging 27.7 points, 12.6 rebounds and 5.2 assists per game this season. Adebayo, who is in his 10th NBA season, all with the Heat, is posting 17.8 points, 8.4 boards, 3.3 assists and a career-high 2.2 steals per outing so far this year. In March, he scored 83 points in a game, the second-most in NBA history.

#2658. Dolphins fan confidence climbs following Lions game, ahead of Week 10 at Colts

The Miami Dolphins have won just one game over the first nine weeks of the 2026 NFL season. Having already passed their bye week, the Dolphins are 1-7, which would typically lead to a frustrated fanbase itching for change. This year, for the Dolphins, however, the fans seem to be accepting that this is only the first season of a long-term rebuild, understanding that losses now may lead to victories in the future.

Things are not all well in Miami, though. This week, two major pieces of the starting lineup landed on injured reserve. Quarterback Malik Willis heads to the sideline for at least four weeks with a groin injury; season-ending surgery hasn't been ruled out, but there is hope he can return in a few weeks. The news was not as good for defensive tackle Zach Sieler, who tore his biceps muscle last week. The injury occurred on the first snap of Miami's game against the Detroit Lions, but the veteran played through it for the rest of the game. Now he will undergo surgery and will need around six months of recovery time before he is cleared to return to football activities.

Miami faces the Indianapolis Colts tomorrow as the NFL's Week 10 schedule continues. Despite Indianapolis struggling this year, earning just two wins against seven losses in the first half of the season, the oddsmakers see the Colts still likely to win this game. The latest FanDuel Sportsbook odds have the Colts favored by 3.5 points over Miami.

The highlight of the season for the Dolphins came in Week 7, when they beat the New York Jets. Our Week 8 SB Nation Reacts fan confidence survey reflected the victory with a 97 percent positive rating for the Dolphins. It was the high-water mark of the year, and the only week in which our survey topped the 90 percent mark.

At least until this week. Going toe-to-toe with the Detroit Lions for much of Week 9 encouraged the fans that brighter days are coming. The survey opened before the news of the injuries to Willis and Sieler broke, but then ran throughout the rest of the week, and it remained up above 90 percent all week. When the poll closed, 91 percent of the responses indicated the Dolphins are headed in the right direction.

How will tomorrow's game against the Colts affect next week's survey? We will be back early next week to ask for your updated confidence in Miami's direction.

#2656. Utah Jazz vs. Houston Rockets: Recap and Final Score

Nov 13, 2026; Houston, Texas, USA; Utah Jazz guard Darryn Peterson (22) handles the ball against Houston Rockets center Alperen Sengun (28) during the first quarter at Toyota Center. Mandatory Credit: Erik Williams-Imagn Images | IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

The Utah Jazz fall to the Houston Rockets 125-118 for their twelfth loss of the season, and second NBA Cup game loss.

The Jazz got off to a solid start in the first quarter with respectable defense and ball movement. Although no one could stop Alpren Sengun, who was getting everything he wanted all night long (including every foul call), Jaxon Hayes and Mo Bamba punished his poor drop coverage on the other end by catching lobs.

After the first quarter, the game was tied up at 27 a piece. Jaxon Hayes came out of the game with an injury (the Jazz can't catch a break with the frontcourt injuries!). Like every other game this season, the bench came in and immediately started turning the ball over and giving up easy baskets, putting the Jazz in a 10-point deficit going into the second half.

Then it got exciting. Darryn Peterson and Keyonte George took over the game, attacking the rim, spraying out the 3-point shooters, and getting stops on the other end. Ace Bailey was hitting his 3s, Keyonte had an amazing 4-point play, and Darryn was controling the game and getting any shot he wanted. Before long, the 10-point deficit became a 10-point lead. Utah's offense was looking as good as it had all season. Darryn Peterson scored 12 points in the first 6 minutes of the third quarter.

But apparently Will Hardy wanted to change things. Enter: Isaiah Collier and Brice Sensabaugh. Instead of going with what was clearly working, Isaiah Collier pounded the air out of the ball as the lead was erased almost as quickly as it was built.

It was about to get ugly again, but Keyonte George came alive, scoring 23 points in the second half. He was aggressive, attacking the basket and creating contact, but the refs decided only the Rockets were allowed to shoot free throws this game.

Bamba wasn't doing anything to stop Sengun, who scored pretty much every time he touched the basketball within 15 feet of the rim. Tony Bradley also played 12 minutes, but didn't do much to stop him either.

However, despite the bench, the refs, and Sengun, the Jazz were still in it. After a beautiful jumper at the elbow from Peterson, the Jazz were down one with 2:40 to go. Amen Thompson scored to put Houston up three, and Keyonte had a terrible turnover, trying to dump the ball down to Bamba. After George's turnover, Sensabaugh bricked an offbalance lay, and Sengun scored on the other end.

With 11 seconds left in the game, the Jazz were down 5. Any reasonable person would have assumed a play would be drawn up for Darryn to score. But no. Not only did that not happen, but he was benched for none of than Svi Mykhailiuk.

Sensabaugh got the ball and did nothing other than jump out of bounds and try to get a foul call.

Another heartbreaking loss in a very winnable game. The bench is horrendous and Will Hardy continues to make mind boggling decisions with the game on the line. Why did we tank all year for Peterson if we are just going to play Brice and Svi at the end of games!?!?

The young core of the Jazz played great, but it wasn't enough. Keyonte, Ace, and Darryn have very bright futures.

Alpren Sengun had 43 points and 12 rebounds.

Kevin Durant did what he does, scoring 22 points without hardly breaking a sweat.

Darryn Peterson finished with 28 points and 7 rebounds for his fifth straight 20-point game. This season has been rough, but at least we have Darryn Peterson.

#2654. Salem football's 'Blue Storm' rolls to sectional title victory

PAULSBORO – Antonio Merendino says every time the Salem High School football team takes the field, it feels like an event.

The Rams have become a huge hit in the postseason and, on Saturday, earned some well-deserved hardware for their performance.

Salem raced to an 18-point lead in the first half and cruised to a 38-14 victory at Paulsboro in Saturday's Central Jersey Group 1 championship game, earning the program's first sectional title since 2022.

More: Everything you need to know for the 2026 high school football season

The Rams (9-3) will play at South Jersey champion Schalick (12-0) in Friday's state semifinal. The winner will play in the state Group 1 final at MetLife Stadium in the Meadowlands the final weekend of November.

"It feels so surreal right now," said Merendino, a defensive lineman who led a stout defense with two sacks. "It looked so rough in the beginning of the season, but we stuck together and this win shows us that the path to a state championship is right in front of us now."

The squad, which calls itself the Blue Storm, weathered some rough seas over the first four weeks of the season that resulted in a 1-3 start. Included in the bumpy stretch was an 8-6 loss to Paulsboro and a 15-14 setback to Schalick, two games that Salem held leads late in the game.

While Salem got some key players into its lineup following the Schalick loss, Merendino points to something else that helped guide the team through the tough start.

"Coach Carr is the code," Merendino said about Salem head coach Kemp Carr. "That man brought us here, he has shown us the way to win. We know what it takes and how to win now.

"It feels like every single game is an event and we're always chasing for something new. We're the Blue Storm, we're coming for it all."

After head coaching stints at Penns Grove and Winslow, Carr returned to his alma mater in 2024. The team went 0-9 that season.

"I've never had a fast pace anywhere I've gone, I've never had the cabinets full," Carr said. "I've always had to build a program. Sometimes it takes time."

Along with the sectional championship trophy, Carr celebrated his 100th career victory, becoming the first coach in South Jersey history to reach the century mark in both basketball and football, according to local historian Chuck Langerman. Carr went 137-68 as Penns Grove's hoops coach while earning 74 wins at the football coach there. He went 11-18 during his stretch at Winslow and is now 15-17 at Salem.

Carr didn't spend much time talking about the milestone, but instead talked about the city he came from.

"It's all about the community," Carr said. "We made this pack and commitment to each other, we wanted to do it for the community. You get a lot of energy from those people, we don't have a lot there, but one thing we do have is football.

"There are really good people there too. We wanted to make sure we didn't let the community down. We didn't want to let the school down."

Salem has become a must-see event over the last two months, winning eight consecutive games and outscoring the opposition 318-53 over the stretch including 114-14 in three postseason contests.

Salem's game plan was pretty simple: Pound the football right at Paulsboro.

The Rams racked up 325 yards on the ground as sophomore Cashmir Parsley ran for 196 yards and three touchdowns while senior quarterback Desmund Thomas added 105 yards. Thomas only attempted two pass plays in the game – completing both for 14 yards.

Parsley has eclipsed the 100-rushing yard mark 10 times this season, including each of the last seven games. He has 1,500 yards for the season to go along with 19 touchdowns.

"He's so special and young too," Carr said about his workhorse running back. "He's just learning how to tote the rock."

Salem's defense was just as productive. Led by a defensive line of Merendino, Myshon Barnes, Raymond Brown and Isiah Carn, the Rams held Paulsboro to 15 rushing yards, recorded four sacks and two takeaways. Kane Green had an interception to close out the first half while David Harris pounced on a fumble on Paulsboro's second-half kickoff return.

Merendino has at least one sack in 11 straight games and has 18.5 for the season.

Salem sophomore Ahmad Tucker returned a punt 45 yards for a touchdown, but was ejected later in the game. He will have to sit out the state semifinal game because of NJSIAA disqualification rules.

Paulsboro finished its season at 9-3. Junior quarterback Jahsir Johnson threw for 174 yards and a touchdown to Julian Gaines. Jeremiah Carr accounted for the Red Raiders' other TD with a 1-yard run.

Tom McGurk is a regional sports editor for the Courier-Post, The Daily Journal and Burlington County Times, covering South Jersey sports for over 40 years. #2653. Falcons vs. Chiefs series history: Win-loss record, matchup results

The Falcons keep the train rolling in AFC play with a date against the Kansas City Chiefs before the bye week. These teams have not met each other often due to the difference in conferences, but in the games where they have faced off, KC has often come out on top with an 8-3 advantage in the head to head record.

The matchups have been more balanced in recent years, but in the early days KC always came out on top, winning the first four matchups, the latter three of those by double digits. These teams only met those four times over a 28 year period between 1972 and 1999.

In 2000, the Falcons finally registered their first victory against the Chiefs, doing it behind Chris Chandler in a quarterback matchup against Elvis Grbac, whose one passing touchdown in the game went to a young Tony Gonzalez. The Chiefs got revenge and then some in the next meeting, in 2004, securing a 56-10 victory in a game where Priest Holmes and Derrick Blaylock each had four rushing touchdowns.

Atlanta finally started settling some scores behind Matt Ryan, who led the Birds to blowout wins in 2008 and 2012, but recent history has things looking a lot more like pre-2000 with three consecutive wins for a Chiefs team that has built a dynasty since their last defeat to Atlanta.

The Kansas City Chiefs were the best team in the league, coming off of back to back Super Bowl titles and on the hunt for a three peat when they went to Atlanta for a date on Sunday Night Football. The upstart Falcons were in the midst of Raheem Morris' first season at the helm and had new quarterback Kirk Cousins leading them.

Things got off to a perfect start for the Falcons when Cousins connected with Drake London for an opening drive touchdown. Patrick Mahomes led the Chiefs on a methodical 17-play drive which ate up over eight minutes, but he was then intercepted by Justin Simmons (who made a habit of picking off Mahomes in his career) in the red zone.

The Chiefs and Falcons traded touchdowns to begin the second quarter before Atlanta's offense went quiet. Atlanta's next three drives were a 3 and out, interception and another 3 and out as KC kicked three field goals and found the end zone via a Juju Smith-Schuster touchdown before the Falcons finally got something going and put up a field goal of their own to make it a one score game again, at 22-17.

From there the defense tightened up, forcing two 3 and outs against the prolific Mahomes, while the offense put together two very long drives that reached the Chief red zone, but they came away with no points turning it over on downs both times. Controversy ensued when it looked like Kyle Pitts was interfered with in the end zone on a 3rd and goal on the first of those drives with no call made. The comeback attempt fell short.

#2651. Warriors vs. Spurs player grades: LJ Cryer shines, Yaxel Lendeborg struggles

Well, that was not a fun game at all. The Golden State Warriors lost 123-85 against the San Antonio Spurs, in a game that was truly over before the Dubs even scored a point. They trailed 18-0 at the halfway mark of the first quarter, and with no Steph Curry to save them, there was never a chance on the road against a team that entered the night with a 10-game winning streak.

No one needs to spend much more time thinking about this game, so let's run through the grades very, very quickly. As always, grades are based on my expectations for each individual player, with a "B" grade representing the average performance for that player.

Note: True-shooting percentage (TS) is a scoring efficiency metric that accounts for threes and free throws. Entering Friday's games, league-average TS was 57.4%.

18 minutes, 8 points, 4 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 1 block, 1 foul, 3-for-11 shooting, 2-for-6 threes, 36.4% TS, -29

Melton got the start in Curry's absence, as I'm guessing he'll continue to do. He did some good things, but also some bad things. His inability to penetrate San Antonio's defense made it hard for him to quarterback the offense, and while he had some nice defensive plays, he struggled with the Spurs' speed and length.

24 minutes, 3 points, 7 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 block, 3 turnovers, 3 fouls, 1-for-13 shooting, 0-for-2 threes, 1-for-2 free throws, 10.8% TS, -40

Well, there's no way to sugarcoat this one, other than to say the obvious: these games happen to rookies, and they're just part of the learning process. Lendeborg will get better because of this game, but he sure won't have a fun time looking back through the film.

This was a double whammy for the Dubs' first-round pick. It was his first game since he was handed the keys following Curry's injury, and it was his first time facing a team like the Spurs. They don't make players like Victor Wembanyama in the Big 10, and they don't make defenses this long, fast, or rangy. Lendeborg was at a total loss when having to deal with those things, though credit to him for not slumping his shoulders, and still playing hard on defense and on the glass.

Grade: D-Post-game bonus: Led the team in rebounds; worst plus/minus on the team.

16 minutes, 4 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 2 turnovers, 3 fouls, 2-for-5 shooting, 0-for-2 threes, 40.0% TS, -23

Green always feels like a barometer for whether or not the team can win. You can tell by his effort level whether it's a winnable game for the Dubs. He came out energized on the first few possessions, while defending Wembanyama, and then it became clear that the Warriors had no chance and Green's effort level waned with that. Can't really blame him.

22 minutes, 8 points, 3 rebounds, 1 block, 2-for-10 shooting, 2-for-5 threes, 2-for-2 free throws, 36.8% TS, -24

He was competitive, which is more than some of his teammates could say, and he fought hard on defense while making a few things happen on offense. It was the kind of performance that stands out when you win, but showed some limitations because they lost.

24 minutes, 20 points, 2 rebounds, 6 assists, 1 steal, 1 foul, 7-for-17 shooting, 6-for-11 threes, 58.8% TS, -11

Cryer was, without a doubt, the brightest part of the game for the Warriors. Maybe the only bright part of the game, for that matter. With Curry and Brandon Williams injured, Cryer — who is on a two-way contract — is going to get some run.

He looked the part in this game. He was part of the first group of subs, and immediately started doing what his teammates had been unable to do: penetrate the defense. Like all the Dubs, he struggled to score once he did that … despite shooting better than 50% from distance, he went just 1-for-6 inside the arc.

The 20 points was a career best for Cryer, and a reminder of what he's capable of with the ball in his hands. The Warriors will need him over the next few weeks.

Single-game individual plus/minus is perhaps the noisiest stat in basketball, but it still feels noteworthy — if not meaningful — that Payton was the only Warriors player to have a positive plus/minus in a game that they lost by 38 points. GPII played some good defense though, which is more than most of his teammates can say. He wasn't a factor on offense, but then again, other than Bassey and Cryer, no one was.

Grade: B-Post-game bonus: Best plus/minus on the team.

Still, with the exception of a bunch of missed shots, Ike played fairly well. He passes really well for his position, and the game doesn't move too quickly for him. Did some nice things on defense as well.

You know something has gone very well or very poorly when there are 14 minutes of garbage time to hand out to the back of the bench. Unfortunately, it was the latter in this case, though at least that meant some extensive play for Leons, who I thought looked pretty darn good.

#2650. Is this level of confidence from Cowboys fans on target?

This has been a rough season for the fans of the Dallas Cowboys. No secret there.

The Cowboys fan base came into the season pretty optimistic. The team had a good offseason with key player personnel pickups, coaching changes, and a surprisingly, for the Cowboys organization, drama-free offseason. With a new defensive scheme under wonderkid Christian Parker, and a holdover offense that was really good the previous season – it was all set up.

In fact, fans had a confidence level of close to 90% (see chart below) when the season began. That lasted all of one week. You can see in the chart below that confidence went up and down with the wins and losses in the early part of the season. But the Tampa Bay Buccaneers loss seemed to break the fan base as confidence never really recovered to over 25% (see below).

Confidence in the direction of the team sits at 11% for this week. But here's the thing. The Cowboys are actually 4-5 on the season, not the worst record in the league. They are still, somewhat, still in playoff contention. The NFC East is still within reach (see second poll answers below).

So is that 11% confidence level too low? What's your call, BTB? Let us know your opinion in the comments below.

We also asked you last week if the Cowboys could realistically win the NFC East. Despite being in second place, only 35% of fans thought that the Cowboys could get it done and make the playoffs.

If they are going to do it, a win on Sunday against the San Francisco 49ers would certainly help. Dallas are currently 4.5-point underdogs to the 49ers.

#2648. Justin Allgaier Says Deciding to Retire Took the Pressure Off in His Title Season

Justin Allgaier says deciding 2026 would be his last NASCAR O'Reilly Auto Parts Series season took the pressure off and made his second title year the most fun of his career.

Allgaier explained it to Will Buxton on SPEED with Harvick and Buxton, in a clip shared by SPEED on FOX.

"I can assure you, a thousand percent, you are on the nail on the head. When I decided I was quitting, it made me realize why I love the sport way more," Allgaier said.

"You get so caught up in the day-to-day and wanting simulator time and study time and being in the gym and doing all these things. When I said I was retiring, it took a pressure off of my shoulders that I was like, this is the best thing that's ever happened to my career," he said.

Allgaier told JR Motorsports in December 2025 that he would step away after the season. He and his family had bought a home in Illinois, and the plan included possibly taking over the family business, D&M Speed Shop, one day.

He said the people around him noticed the difference.

"And everybody kept saying, hey, I don't understand. You do realize you have a championship to go win this weekend, right? Like, you were not nervous. You just seem free," Allgaier said.

"I'm like, yeah, I mean, I was retiring. This is money in the bank. This is house money right here. So I get to go have fun and enjoy it. And I think that that's been the catalyst for what's made this year so much fun," he said.

The change also coincided with a career year. Allgaier won six regular-season races, a career best, and entered the Chase as the top seed.

His title run was not smooth. Trouble at Gateway and a crash at Las Vegas briefly dropped him behind Sheldon Creed in the standings.

He recovered with top-five finishes at Charlotte, Phoenix, Talladega and Martinsville. He went to Homestead-Miami Speedway with a 22-point lead over Jesse Love.

Allgaier finished seventh and clinched his second title in three seasons. It was the fifth drivers' championship for JR Motorsports, which Dale Earnhardt Jr. co-owns with his sister, Kelley Earnhardt Miller, and Rick Hendrick.

By then, the retirement plan had changed. Allgaier said in May that his plans were no longer certain, and he later signed a multiyear deal to stay in the No. 7 Chevrolet.

Kelley Earnhardt Miller has also weighed in on the extension. Earnhardt Jr., meanwhile, said this week that he wanted to tell Allgaier to calm down during the finale but worried about a penalty.

The 40-year-old will chase a third O'Reilly Series title in 2027. This time, he will not be racing as if every week is his last in racing, a mindset he credits for making 2026 so much fun.

#2647. Castle, Spurs rout Warriors for 11th straight win

Planes were grounded nationwide on Friday night and the San Antonio Spurs grounded the Golden State Warriors from the opening tip in their second NBA Cup game. Fueled by Stephon Castle, the Spurs took a 20-point first quarter lead and never looked back as they defeated the Warriors 123-85 at the Frost Bank Center.

The victory is the 11th straight for San Antonio, who also improved to 2-0 in NBA Cup play and control their own destiny to advance to the knockout stage of the competition. The Spurs would need only one win in their next two Cup games to book a spot in the next round.

Nothing the Stephen Curry-less Warriors tried on Friday worked against the Spurs, who led by as many as 43 points in the game. Stephon Castle rebounded from a few poor outings in the last few games to score a season-high 32 points to go along with six assists and five rebounds. Castle shot 12-of-18 from the field as he scored 13 of the Spurs' first 26 points.

"He's in the middle of everything," Coach Johnson said of Castle. "He's an unbelievable impact player. He's never blinked at anything we've asked. He's never shown any poor body language. He just attacks everything. And there are times in this league, that is ridiculously difficult."

Early on, it was obvious what type of night this was going to be for the Spurs. San Antonio scored the first 18 points of the game and did not allow Golden State to score their first points until there was 5:54 left in the opening period. The Spurs' defense was swarming all through the first quarter, getting hands into the passing lanes and by the time the buzzer sounded to end the period, the Warriors only had 14 points were shooting 21 percent.

"I think the defense probably ignites the offense more than vice versa," Johnson said. "So I still lean into the defense. Thought we were pretty on point with some of our game plan execution stuff and attention to detail."

Golden State's standout rookie Yaxel Lendeborg struggled, shooting just 1-for-13 and ended with the game just three points. Credit to Victor Wembanyama and Luke Kornet, who both worked to make life difficult for the rookie.

"Nobody wanted to go into the paint," Lendeborg said. "Nobody wanted to get their shot blocked by him. So we were all just doing some weak-ass floaters. I don't know how many of us work on those.

San Antonio won all four quarters, but a 34-27 third quarter forced Coach Johnson to sit the starters and put his bench in with the Spurs comfortably ahead by 37 points. The majority of the fourth quarter saw Tarris Reed Jr., Carter Bryant, Jordan McLaughlin, and Keldon Johnson take the floor in mop up duty.

The win also saw the return of Devin Vassell, who missed the previous two games with adductor tightness. De'Aaron Fox was questionable with soreness, but he also took the floor in the win for the Spurs.

Victor Wembanyama had a quiet 12 points and 12 rebounds, but he was also hit in the throat, poked in the eye, and thrown to the floor by Draymond Green. After the game, Wemby commented on this and said he was "not surprised" by Green's flagrant foul.

De'Aaron Fox did not have his shot on Friday, going just 1-of-7 from the floor. However, he made up for that by doing the little things. He had five rebounds, four assists to contribute in a different way.

Keldon Johnson is starting to look like the man who won Sixth Man of the Year last season. He scored 18 points off the bench to go along with four rebounds and four assists in 25 minutes. If he can do that consistently, it makes the Spurs even more dangerous than they already are.

The Spurs (11-2) will now hit the road to face the Utah Jazz at the Delta Center for the final time this season on Sunday afternoon at 4 p.m. central time.

#2645. Previewing the New York Jets’ opposition: Buffalo Bills

The last time the Jets faced the Buffalo Bills, they lost 35-8 despite the fact that the Bills were resting all of their starters. New York has a stronger team right now than they had at the end of last year, but there's still a big gulf between them and the 7-1 Bills.

On Sunday, the Jets will be heavy underdogs as the Bills seek to continue setting the pace in the race for the AFC's number one seed.

Let's break down the Bills' roster going position by position:

With Josh Allen once again leading the way, the Bills have the league's number one offense in terms of points and yardage.

Allen has been solid, but his numbers are not as impressive as in some of his previous seasons. He hasn't thrown for 300 yards since the opener and his quarterback rating is below 100.

He's also thrown six interceptions in the past six games, which is uncharacteristic for a player who had just 16 interceptions in his past 43 regular season and playoff games prior to this. He does have a career-best (and league-leading) yards per attempt average of 8.6 though.

The backup is veteran Kyle Allen, who is 7-12 as a starter in his career.

The only change on the offensive line this year for the Bills is that Alec Anderson has replaced David Edwards at left guard. The other four starters remain from a group that all started at least 15 regular or postseason games last year. The versatile Anderson had also played over 200 snaps off the bench.

The other four starters include five-time pro bowler Dion Dawkins at left tackle and 2024 pro bowler Connor McGovern at center. On the right side, tackle Spencer Brown and guard O'Cyrus Torrence round out the starting group.

The Bills have been banged up on the line, though. Dawkins and Anderson missed their last game against the Vikings on Monday night football, Brown missed practice on Thursday and McGovern was also limited in practice. Spencer has been listed as doubtful, but the other four starters are due to play.

On the bench, the Bills have Tyler Grable, who has started a game at each tackle position this year and veteran Austin Corbett who started in place of Anderson on Sunday. The backup center is Lloyd Cushenberry and they also have two rookies – fourth rounder Jude Bowry and seventh rounder Ar'maj Reed-Adams.

The Bills have the number two running game in the NFL, led by James Cook, who is averaging over 100 rushing yards per game. Cook has also rushed for eight touchdowns, although that's actually one less than Allen.

Backup Ray Davis hasn't featured much but should be confident having had a monster game against the Jets in January.

The third down back is still ex-Jet Ty Johnson who has really found himself a home with the Bills. Johnson has 17 receptions off the bench.

The Bills' biggest star at wide receiver is DJ Moore, but he's been slowed by injuries since posting a 100-yard game in the season opener. Instead, they've been led by Khalil Shakir, who has 31 catches. Keon Coleman has also contributed with 23 receptions and a team-high three touchdown catches.

Tight end Dalton Kincaid leads the Bills in receiving yards with 429 as Dawson Knox, a 2022 pro bowler, continues to play second fiddle.

The other backups at wide receiver – fourth round rookie Skyler Bell and ex-Jets return specialist Greg Dortch – have yet to register a catch.

Reserve tight ends Jackson Hawes and Keleki Latu have just three catches between them, but Latu's only reception was a 25-yard touchdown.

New defensive coordinator and ex-Jets safety Jim Leonhard has switched the Bills' defense to a 3-4 system this year and they're in the middle of the pack overall but actually strong against the run and weak against the pass, statistically.

The massive Deone Walker has arguably been the biggest beneficiary of the scheme change on the interior, continuing to emerge as a disruptive pass rusher in addition to the space he takes up against the run. He's also batted down five passes at the line.

The other two starters are Ed Oliver and TJ Sanders, although they've missed seven games between them so backups Greg Gaines, DeWayne Carter and Landon Jackson have all also seen extensive playing time.

Oliver has been productive when he has played, with 10 quarterback hits in just five games.

Whether it was due to the scheme change or because he was just due to break out anyway, 2026 has been the year that Gregory Rousseau has taken his game to a new level coming off the edge.

Rousseau, who led the team with seven sacks last year, already has eight sacks in eight games to tie his career high. In addition, he has eight passes defensed which more than doubles the most he has ever had in a season.

Otherwise, Buffalo doesn't have anyone with two sacks, although the other starting edge, Bradley Chubb, has 1.5 sacks and eight quarterback hits.

The starters inside are Dorian Williams and Terrel Bernard. Bernard plays a full-time role and leads the team with 57 tackles but Williams has two interceptions to lead the team. Fourth round rookie Kaleb Elarms-Orr has also seen action in coverage situations.

Veteran Michael Hoecht and second round rookie TJ Parker are the main backups on the edge, with Javon Solomon and Mike Danna having not played much.

Joe Andresseen has only played on special teams so far, but is the number four inside linebacker.

The Bills' number one cornerback is Christian Benford but the other starter has been a surprise package. Second round rookie Davison Igbinosun currently leads the team with nine pass breakups.

In the slot, the Bills let longtime slot cornerback Taron Johnson go during the offseason and replaced him with another experienced player in Dee Alford.

Last year's first round pick Maxwell Hairston has still received plenty of playing time on the outside but is behind the rookie right now. He may need to step up this week because Benford is listed as questionable with a wrist injury despite practicing full on Friday.

The starting safeties are Damar Hamlin and Cole Bishop. CJ Gardner-Johnson had been starting but was released after getting suspended following a controversial spitting incident.

Sam Franklin and fifth round rookie Jalon Kilgore are available as cover at safety, as is Amani Hooker, who was just acquired via trade. Te'Cory Couch is also on the roster as cornerback cover.

After missing the entire 2025 season due to injury, Tyler Bass is having a stellar 2026 season as he has made all 19 of his field goal attempts, including a game-winner against the Rams. He has missed two extra points though.

Their punter is a rookie, seventh rounder Tommy Doman. So far, his numbers are underwhelming but he hasn't allowed much in the way of return yardage.

Davis, who was an all-pro kick returner a few years ago, splits kickoff return duties with Dortch, who is also the main punt returner. Davis had a 70-yarder and Dortch had a 58-yarder so far on kickoffs.

Alford and Shakir are also options on punts and Johnson is the third-stringer on kickoffs.

Andreessen, Franklin and Hoecht have been Buffalo's most productive players in kick coverage. Andreessen was second in the league with 23 special teams tackles last season. Franklin is their primary punt gunner, although he has been listed as questionable with a shoulder issue.

#2644. 5 things we learned after the Ohio State victory over Northwestern

It certainly wasn't a smooth walk through among the roses, but Ohio State got back in the win column after dropping two straight games when it beat Northwestern 28-19 on Saturday. The only time the Buckeyes trailed was when the Wildcats scored first on a field goal in the first quarter, but from there the Buckeyes had the lead despite several miscues and mental mistakes that surely frustrated all of Buckeye Nation.

In fact, the score made things look a little closer than it was after Ohio State fumbled while running the clock out, and then the Wildcats took the ball down and scored a touchdown on the last play of the game (sorry, bettors). At the end of the day, it was a win OSU needed badly just to get some positive momentum with a freshman running the show under center, and now it's time to build from here. Jeremiah Smith even added another record to his personal achievements.

After each Ohio State football game, we like to reflect on what we saw and what we can take away from the action, and so it is here too. Here are five things we think we learned about the Buckeyes after their 28-19 victory over Northwestern on Saturday.

Some of the mistakes we're seeing from this team are baffling, especially this late in the season. There are a lot of new guys becoming part of the culture and a lot of young guys trying to figure things out, but the list is pretty long on how many times the Buckeyes didn't keep their composure or simply made a bad play physically.

From Isaiah West fumbling the ball away while Ohio State was looking to run out the clock, to Legend Bey taunting the Northwestern sideline after he made several ridiculous moves for an eight-yard game, it made the game closer than it needed to be and could have spelled doom against a more talented opponent.

At this point, the team kind of is what it is, and I'm not sure these mistakes can all of a sudden turn off, but I guess we'll see.

Freshman backup quarterback Luke Fahey did show more composure this game as he continues to find his footing at one of the glamour positions of college football. It wasn't perfect by any means, but he seems to be gaining more confidence. Yes, he missed several throws and reads, but he finished the day 21-of-32 for 201 yards with three touchdowns and an interception.

Those stats aren't as good (arguably) as what he had in his first two starts, but he played better by looking for Jeremiah Smith more and by not taking so many check-downs. Ohio State still needs him to play even better, especially in Lincoln next week against Nebraska, but it was a step in the right direction.

That was more like it for the Ohio State defense. The late score made things look worse as the Buckeyes were playing softer coverage, but giving up 19 points after allowing 30 and 42 against USC and Oregon, respectively, is more of what we've come to expect from the OSU defense and Matt Patricia.

Northwestern was the second-leading rushing team in the Big Ten, and Ohio State allowed just 53 total yards on the ground in this one. And while the Wildcats have more success through the air than you'd expect, the fact that the offense and special teams put the OSU defense in a bad spot on more than one occasion, and the reality that Chip Kelly was on the other sideline, means we say good job in this one.

Yes, No. 4 is still the best player in college football, and we're glad to see that the Ohio State coaching staff decided to get in Fahey's ear and remind him of that. Smith had a lot more targets than last week and finished with 11 receptions for 117 yards (10.6 ypc) and a touchdown. Those aren't eye-popping, but they are very good numbers that should allow him to be on top of the Heisman race.

I always hated how voters cast their votes for the Heisman by putting too much emphasis on teams winning, the stats, and the position the player plays. In this case, there's little doubt that Smith is the best player in college football, so just make sure he gets to New York and wins the thing. There's a lot of attention being paid to him on a weekly basis, and he's still putting up better numbers than almost anyone else.

Putting the Scarlet and Gray uniform on is like being in a fishbowl. You are expected to win every game, sometimes by multiple scores, and when any of those two things don't happen, the local media and fans are all over this team. Heck, the Buckeyes hadn't lost three regular-season games and two in a row since 2011, so things are at a fever pitch in Columbus with head coach Ryan Day, the coaching staff, players, and maybe even the water boy coming under the gun.

By watching this game, it sure feels like the players are feeling it. There were several dropped passes or interceptions, some mental mistakes, and guys trying to do too much instead of relaxing and just doing their jobs and allowing the coaching and talent of others to take over. It feels like how Ohio State played against Michigan for about a three-year run, and there's no doubt the players are feeling the pinch of criticism. The men in the arena need to put that aside and just play free and loose.

Contact/Follow us @BuckeyesWire on X (formerly Twitter) and like our page on Facebook to follow ongoing coverage of Ohio State news, notes, and opinion. Follow Phil Harrison on X.

This article originally appeared on Buckeyes Wire: 5 things we learned after the Ohio State victory over Northwestern

#2642. Highlights: San Antonio wastes no time blowing out the Warriors

There are good starts… and then there are very good starts. An 18-0 run to begin the game against one of the best offenses in the league qualifies as an outstanding start. The San Antonio Spurs built a 38-14 first-quarter lead over the Golden State Warriors on their way to a 123-85 win.

The Spurs were led in large part by the stellar offensive play of Stephon Castle. The guard is coming off a stretch of bad play, but turned it around in a major way against the Warriors. Castle had 32 points, 6 assists, and 5 rebounds. He had it going from deep, hitting a trio of three-pointers in the first quarter, and finished the game shooting 5-for-7 from three.

Castle did more than just hit open threes. The Warriors had no answer for his physical, downhill brand of basketball. With Draymond Green attached at Victor Wembanyama's hip, Castle and the Spurs guards got to the basket with little to no resistance at the rim. San Antonio outscored Golden State 56-to-28 in the paint.

Castle's passing was on point against the Warriors. He had no turnovers despite having the ball in his hands for a lot of the game. He was particularly effective on the primary and secondary fast breaks, where Golden State was slow to get back.

Castle and Wembanyama had the Area 51 connection working early and often. Wembanyama wasn't able to get free in the half-court too often with Green playing physical basketball, but San Antonio was able to get him a few lobs out of the pick-and-roll.

It wasn't just Wembanyama receiving lobs from Castle; he also delivered them. Sometimes it's easy to take this stuff for granted because of all the amazing things that Wembanyama can do… but this is your 7-foot-5 center dribbling the ball up and throwing an underhand lob to his point guard for a reverse slam. That is versatility in one play! Wembanyama ended the game with 12 points, 12 rebounds, 6 assists, and 2 blocks.

To further add insult to injury, the duo did it again just a few minutes later!

Early in the game, the Spurs ran a lot of pick-and-rolls with Wembanyama handling the ball, and their guards setting picks. The Warriors sent doubles at Wembanyama on these plays, making it easy for the guards to slip into the paint and create easy looks. Here, Dylan Harper is able to get to the basket and finish over Golden State's smaller defenders.

Golden State left the paint essentially wide open all game long. It felt like the Spurs could touch the lane whenever they wanted to. Harper carved up the defense with his tight handle for a nifty finish in the second quarter. Harper had a quiet but efficient game, scoring 9 points on 4-of-8 shooting.

Harper also nailed a transition three on one of the most memorable plays of the night. Shortly after the ball went into the basket, Green shoulder-checked Wembanyama into the ground and received a flagrant foul.

Wembanyama finished the game with seemingly no health issues despite the hard hit. He and the rest of the Spurs will get a day off before a rematch with the Utah Jazz on Sunday as they look to win their twelfth game in a row.

#2641. NFL tells Senator that Jalen Hurts review will include "mutually agreed-upon independent medical experts"

Five weeks since Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts played a game in London with a blood clot in his leg, the NFL and NFL Players Association have not yet begun an official investigation of the situation.

As noted on Friday by Tim McManus of ESPN, the league and the union are still working “to hammer out [the] parameters” of the investigation.

That has not stopped the NFL from providing some preliminary information to Senator Lisa Blunt Rochester (D-Del.), who previously expressed concerns about the situation in a letter to the league.

“As outlined by the league and NFLPA’s Collective Bargaining Agreement, mutually agreed-upon independent medical experts will review the matter and share their conclusions and recommendations with both parties,” NFL executive vice president of communications, public affairs and health policy Jeff Miller said in a statement that was released by Blunt Rochester’s office on Friday.

It’s still unclear how the investigation will proceed or what it will entail. The NFLPA has not yet invoked its absolute right to launch an investigation under Article 39 of the Collective Bargaining Agreement as the communications with the league continue.

The ensuing delay is approaching the status of “head scratching.” With games being played every Thursday, Sunday, and Monday, there’s a constant risk of a similar situation arising. The sooner the Hurts situation is properly explored and any lessons learned from it are put into action, the sooner all players will benefit from any enhanced protections.

The Eagles and the league created widespread skepticism regarding whether a coverup is happening. (The NFL may not appreciate that being said, but that doesn’t make the perception inaccurate.) The NFLPA can’t afford to be viewed as being complicit in an effort to sweep the incident under the rug.

Through it all, the most effective investigative process comes from litigation, an adversarial process aimed at getting to the truth. However it happens as to Hurts, the only goal should be getting to the truth about why it all happened — and how it can be prevented from happening again.
