#2640. Take it to the bank: How Bradley Braves upset George Mason in college basketball duel

ST. PETERSBURG, Fla. — Xander Alarie took a page from Paul Pierce to author an upset story for the Bradley Braves on Saturday at the Showdown in St. Pete.

Alarie completed a Bradley comeback from eight points down in the second half to stun George Mason, 62-61, on a game-winning shot with 2 seconds left at McArthur Center on the campus of Eckerd College.

The Braves, 13.5-point underdogs against the physically bigger, undefeated Patriots, put the ball in Alarie's hands at the end.

He battled against a double-team on the right edge of the paint, maintained control, and fought up a shot from the right elbow that banked in for the lead.

Bradley then survived a desperate 3-point heave at the buzzer by George Mason to win its second straight game in Florida and post the Braves' signature victory so far this season.

"Like Paul Pierce said, I call bank, called game," said Alarie, joking, in postgame interview from Florida. "The play was drawn up for me to get the ball in the post and make a read. I thought I had to take a shot. I thought it was the right play. We executed well and got it done.

"Last game (Bradley's win Friday over Coppin State) I wasn't happy with myself. I got in foul trouble both these games. I talked to my coaches and felt I had to respond. They told me they needed me, I need to step up and make plays, take shots and win games for my team.

Bradley guard Trevon Peyton made consecutive 3-pointers 36 seconds apart for a 38-35 lead with 15:18 left.

But George Mason answered with a 12-2 run over the next 2:38, including three straight 3-pointers from Shane Pendergrass, to punch out a 47-40 lead.

The Braves trailed by as much as eight points with 6:33 remaining before making their final run.

A 3-pointer by Payton off a turnover pulled Bradley within 61-60 with two minutes to go.

An exchange of turnovers followed, and Bradley sent Efeosa Oliogu-Elabor to the line for a one-and-one with 14 seconds left.

He missed the front end of it to open the door for Bradley. Timoty van der Knaap grabbed the rebound to set up the Braves for their final shot from Alarie.

"We kept fighting, believed in ourselves," Payton said. "Keep fighting, keep fighting. Coach Wardle told us at halftime to keep fighting, and that's what we did. We kept chipping away, chipping away …

"Felt like every other play was a dogfight."

The Braves improved to 3-2. … George Mason slipped to 3-1. … BU forward Xander Alarie had a game-high 17 points on 6 of 8 shooting, including 3 of 3 from 3-point range. He added 5 rebounds. … BU guard Trevon Payton had 13 points in 20 minutes, playing with four fouls. … Bradley was 9 of 21 (42.9%) from 3-point range and perfect on the free throw line at 15 of 15. … George Mason's Emmanuel Kanga had a double-double on 11 points and 13 rebounds. … The Braves had their hands full against a bigger George Mason team, which won the paint, 32-20, and the boards, 36-25. … Next up for Bradley is a 7 p.m. game against Purdue-Fort Wayne on Wednesday in Carver Arena. ... Bradley guard and Peoria product Dietrich Richardson was wearing a protective boot on his right foot and was among the non-uniformed players on the BU bench.

#2638. Inside the Suns: Collin Gillespie, early-season player trades, the deep bench players

Welcome to Inside the Suns, a deep-dive analysis of the current Phoenix Suns team in which the Fantable — a round table of Bright Siders — give their takes on the Suns' latest issues and news.

Author's Note: All questions were sent out to the Fantable members before the Suns/Clippers game was played.

zenzino: We got these questions right after the Spurs game. My answer was 'yes, I am concerned'. Gillespie had a tough final 3rd of last season, and he kinda picked up where he left off. He's not bad; he's just not as good as he was the first half of last season.

The other effect of him not shooting well is he's passing up shots he should be taking, which sometimes leads to ill-advised passes.

I've seen some comments to the effect of 'he's got his contract, so now he's coasting'. I call BS. You don't fight through the things he had to fight through and call it good when you get your first contract. He's a competitor.

Over the last two games, it seems like he's finding his range more. It's only two games, but there is some progress. As the 3s go down more, his confidence will grow. He'll take shots he should be taking and hopefully hit key shots like we know he can. So, I'm a little less concerned now than I was at the beginning of the week.

Ashton: It is funny in that there are a lot of players not playing offensively well, so just to call out Gillespie is a bit hypocritical. Yes, three-point shooting is abysmal across the board, and Gillespie is not helping his case there. But look at Kennard vs. Charlotte. He went 0-for-5 from the perimeter as the Sun's backup sharpshooter but still carved a role out facilitating and making mid-court jumpers.

So, I am not overly concerned about Gillespie as long as he finds a role on the team as the games go on. And that role is to probably assist Booker as much as possible. Would a starter role help pull him out of a funk and make him consistent? Perhaps, and the season is still young enough to experiment with

OldAz: I think Collin knew you were asking this and intentionally broke out of his slump the last couple of games. But even before these last couple of games, he had one of the best on/off splits on the team among players that play significant minutes (behind Dunn, of course, who has one of the best on/off splits in the league).

Considering Gillespie plays point guard, I care much more about how the team plays with him on the floor than I do about his individual offensive numbers.  The same goes for Dunn, who really needs to start getting more minutes because the Suns play significantly better when he is on the floor despite his lack of counting stats. Really, this goes for all players, as it's far more enjoyable to watch Suns games when the team is playing well, rather than watching a "fantasy"-style game where individual stats matter more. As it relates to Gillespie, it was only a matter of time before his shots started falling. He is far too good a shooter to stay that low for long.

Rod: He did have me a little concerned until the Clippers game. Before that, he was making less than 30% of his threes and hitting on only slightly better than that from the field…and Oso had a better FT percentage (46.2%) than him (25%)! His percentages are way up in the past two games, which is a relief. Thankfully, his defensive intensity never left, and he seems to be back to having a positive impact on both sides of the court again.

zenzino: 'NOW' would be stupid. There is a reason there is no trade activity this early in the season. After December 15th, many more players become available for trade, so nothing will happen before then.

All these fantasy GMs are trade-happy. It takes (at least) two teams to trade. Another team has to want you; you've got to be willing to give up someone you want. These things don't happen easily.

By all accounts, a great guy and a better teammate. His playmaking has shown definite improvement, and he has bought into doing the things they're asking of him. Unfortunately, because he's being asked to do something completely different, he's not playing his game, and we're not seeing how good he can actually be. I've been a big proponent of giving it some time. But I'm not super optimistic. I think he can be a pretty great player in the right situation, and the Suns might not be that situation. I would love to see him excel, even if it has to be on another team.

Many fans didn't want him here in the first place. While he's been improving, it's not a steady climb. It still looks a little clunky. This is the first time he's been on a different team, in a different system, and having to adjust his game. That takes time, and I definitely think it's too soon to decide if he's going to work out or not.

"He's playing great. Trade him for picks while his value is high." Unfortunately, there's a subset of 'fans' who don't really understand basketball. I invite them to go be Lakers fans. They'll fit right in.

I have actually seen several posts saying it's time to trade Brooks. SMH. Fans can be a fickle bunch.

Should the Suns be putting out feelers after December 15th? Absolutely. See what's available. But it has to be a trade that makes a difference. They don't need to force a trade and settle for what they can get. There is no player on this team that is an 'addition by subtraction' player. If there is an opportunity to improve the roster, then they should definitely take it seriously. If nothing good comes to fruition, let it ride.

Ashton: Certain Suns players…eh? Gee, based on board comments, that could be literally anyone. Well, scratch Book. He is literally the only one carrying the Suns on his back. Of course, there are things to consider such as aprons and when players can be traded, but as far as I am concerned, I really do not think anyone on the squad, outside of Book, is untradeable. There has been a lot of focus on Green as the asset to be traded, but once that includes a pick, and don't even ask me what the status of Sun's picks is, nope. Pass.

This is my fluffy positive comment that some members on the board say is lacking. This is like a Back to the Future movie where the Suns are doing what they did last season. They are winning the games they should (Houston anomaly aside) and losing the games that probably would have. And to add more cheer to your day, the Suns still have the Kings four times and the Clippers another 3. Suns could be a play-in team!

So no, there is no need for a knee-jerk reaction quite yet. Particularly with the Lakers up next. I am still on the side of letting everyone gel together organically before the fans tear down the team and front office decisions.

OldAz: I believe they should be fielding calls from any team that covets one of the Suns "stars" (outside of Booker), but they also should not be hasty to move anyone just because they have gotten off to a slow start.  I would not include any young players or trade more draft picks, but if someone wants to offer the Suns a combination of expiring contracts, young players, and picks for a player like Brooks or Green, I would not hang up the phone.

Rod: Not really. Most teams aren't looking to make trades this early on, and I think the best return you could hope for might be an expiring contract or two. Giving up on a player this early sends a message to other GMs that you're likely desperate, and they're going to lowball the return they're offering (plus almost every team has at least one or two players that aren't available to be traded yet). Now if another team calls about making a trade, that changes the equation, giving the Suns a bit more leverage, and they should certainly listen, as it puts them in a better position to bargain.

And we have to keep in mind that the Suns have six players on their roster that currently can't be traded (Brooks, Williams, Gillespie, Goodwin, Kennard, and Ighodaro), which complicates things.

zenzino: Jamaree Bouyea. Hands down. Especially in a game like the Spurs game. I would have liked to see Jamaree in there in the 2nd quarter when it was obvious no one on the team not named Booker could get a bucket. What Goodie does for the defense, Jamaree does for the offense. He brings instant energy on both ends of the floor. And he can score. What he lacks in size, he makes up for in speed and energy.

Like everyone else, I'd like to know what's going on with Sheer. I know Ott wanted him ready for the Blazers game, but he hurt his ankle. It doesn't look like the Suns are going to blow out many teams, but they might get blown out more than we'd like. Still, it would be a shame just to see Sheer play in garbage time. It's hard for young players to get into any rhythm with that kind of playing time.

Ashton: Ryan Dunn only played ten minutes against Charlotte. Does he count? True, he may be injured, and that would explain it. The obvious answer is to give Koa Peat some run, if only to shut me up. It is not like Bridges and Brooks is setting the world on fire, so why not? Peat can clear a path for Booker while facilitating his touches. I mean, common, teams are now throwing triple teams at Booker. This is Koa Peat's time to shine in a role he knows well from his college days. I figure any other name to throw out there might be in the doghouse or incompatible with Coach Ott's thinking, but…there is that guy down in G-League named Drew Timme that could be elevated to the end of the bench role and utilized correctly.

OldAz: As noted before, I think Dunn has earned more minutes and fits well when multiple starters are on the court because he does not need the ball on offense and brings energy and effort on defense that the starters lack and can be infectious. However, he technically is getting some "rotation" minutes already.

The only real choices here are Fleming or Peat, based on their positional fit and long-term upside. I would always be happy to see them get a few meaningful minutes per game and expand those as they earn them. More important to me would be seeing current rotation bench players get more minutes when they are playing well, and mixing them in more with the current starters.  The Suns have been playing better as of late, so I would not rush to force minutes on Fleming or Peat unless they earn them, but I would totally be in favor of continuing to experiment with lineups and riding the hot hand (on offense or defense) far more than Ott has done in the past.

Rod: Here's my wild idea for the day…start Jamaree Bouyea at point guard and bench (or trade) Green. I really like the idea of Collin staying on the second unit, and Bouyea actually seems to be the better driver and finisher at the rim than Collin, and is also a little faster and more athletic. I'd just like to see this tried, as I think Bouyea could be a much better fit with Book, Brooks, Bridges, and Ighodaro (or Maluach) than Green. I don't dislike Green and don't necessarily want him traded, but I also don't like the idea of him taking a spot on the 2nd unit from any of the guys already there because they already play well together, and I wouldn't want to mess that up (which is another reason I'd rather keep Collin there instead of moving him up to the starting unit).

Second, Peat should start getting some meaningful rotation minutes soon. Right now, I wouldn't bench anyone just to get him those minutes, but if someone just needs some rest (or they have a hangnail, or the sniffles, or…I'm sure you catch my drift here), then sit that player and get Peat into the rotation for at least one game.

Finally, if there's even the slightest need for Oso or Khaman to sit out a game, activate Timme and get him some minutes with the main team. Sure, his averages in the G League won't directly transfer to the NBA level, but a guy averaging 32.5 ppg, 13.0 rebounds, and 7.0 assists while shooting 42.9% from three on 3.5 attempts per game sounds like someone who should get a chance to me. Reath hasn't been very impressive as the Suns' 3rd center so why not let Timme have his shot at earning that slot?

As always, many thanks to our Fantable members for all their extra effort this week!

On November 17, 2000, Jason Kidd tied the NBA record for turnovers in a game when he turned the ball over 14 times in a 90-85 loss to New York. John Drew of the Atlanta Hawks previously held the record alone after committing 14 turnovers at New Jersey on March 1, 1977. This record still stands today.

On November 20, 1977, the Suns set a franchise record for team steals in a single game with 24 in a 134-112 win against an Antonio. That total is tied for the 4th most in a single game in NBA history. Starters Walter Davis and Alvan Adams both had 4, Paul Westphal and Don Buse getting 3 each, and Gar Heard finished with 1. Rookie Ron Lee had 5 off the bench to lead everyone in steals, though. Lee made a name for himself as a thief on the court and went on to set the Suns' single-season record for steals (225) the following season, as well as leading the entire NBA in steals that year.

On November 20, 2000, due to various injuries and declining performance, Rex Chapman retired at the age of 32. Chapman spent the final four years of his NBA career with the Suns averaging 12.4 points, 2.5 assists and 2-4 rebounds per game for the Suns while shooting 36.0% from three. He started 154 of the 224 games he played in Phoenix. He is 14th on the Suns' All-Time list for career 3-pointers made (324), 18th on the All-Time list for threes made per game (1.45), and 50th on the Suns' All-Time list of leading scorers (2,787 points).

#2636. Open Thread: Spurs and Celtics stand out in Emirates Cup competition

Last night the Spurs torched the Golden State Warriors. Until then, the two were the remaining undefeated teams in West Group C. As it stands now, each group has one undefeated team. In addition to the Spurs, the Mavericks and Timberwolves are 2-0 in their respective Western Conference groupings.

In the East, the Magic, Heat, and Celtics also have 2-0 records. Of the six undefeated teams, the Spurs and Celtics currently hold 53-point differentials. There are three more days of Group competition — Friday, November 20th, the following Wednesday and wrapping up two days later on Friday, November 27th.

From there, the eight top teams will be determined for the quarterfinals. The top team from each group advance leaving one additional spot. Those with matching records will organized by point differential. The Spurs and Celtics have a distinct advantage at this point in the standings.

The Spurs made it all the way to the Emirates Cup Finals last year. They knocked out the LeBron and Luka Lakers before upsetting the reigning NBA Champion Oklahoma City Thunder. They did fall short in the Finals to the New York Knicks. Although they outscored the Knicks in the 1st and 3rd quarters and tied the second, they unraveled in the final frame. The same issues plagued them and lead to their demise in the NBA Finals a few months later.

For now, the Spurs seem to have a handle on their competition. But there are always bumps in the road. Fortunately, this season, smoothing the edges has been a key to their success. Making adjustments in real time has given them separation in many games, even allowing a monster comeback last week when the Chicago Bulls started with an uncharacteristic 85% shooting during the first quarter. It is in these moments that the Spurs have been tested and shown they are more ready than ever to rise to the challenge.

The Spurs next Emirates Cup games is Wednesday, November 25 against the Portland Trail Blazers with their final game against the Lakers on Friday, November 27.

#2635. Three-peat: Malone women win third straight NCCAA XC national title

JOPLIN, MO. — It's a three-peat for the Malone University women's cross country team.

The Pioneers won their third straight team title at the National Christian College Athletic Association Championships at Missouri Southern University on Nov. 13. Malone head coach Zach Fresenko also received NCAA Coach of the Year honors.

Rachel Whetsone finished sixth in 18:49 and Elena Price came in ninth in 19:07 to lead the Pioneers. Both received NCCAA All-American honors. Price, a freshman, sliced nearly a minute off her season best.

With their top runners home preparing for next week's NCAA Division II regional, the Pioneers still finished second in the men's race.

Malone was led by All-Americans Tesfaye Young and Brylan Holland. Young finished sixth in 25:14. Holland placed ninth in 25:19.

#2632. Ronaldo-less Portugal suffer Nations League hammering at hands of Denmark

Portugal were hammered 4-1 by Denmark on Saturday, an embarrassing Nations League defeat in their first match without Cristiano Ronaldo following the superstar forward's retirement from international football.

Rasmus Hojlund starred with a superb brace as Denmark moved into second place in Group A4 and closed in on a spot in the quarter-finals with a brilliant display in Braga.

The Danes are on nine points from five fixtures, three behind Portugal who were already sure of qualification for the last eight having won all four of their previous group fixtures.

A draw against third-placed Norway on Tuesday, 3-1 winners over Wales earlier, will see Denmark through.

Ahead of kick-off Portugal paid tribute to Ronaldo, who announced his exit from the international game last week, with the team and mascots walking out wearing Ronaldo's number seven and his name on their jerseys.

Fans held up a huge banner which read "Thank you captain" as the teams entered the field, while applause rolled around the stadium once the match clock hit seven.

Ronaldo made his debut for Portugal in 2003 and went on to establish world records for goals and appearances in international football, netting 146 times in 234 matches.

The highlight of his international career was winning Euro 2016, but it ended badly when the 41-year-old fell out with Portugal coach Jorge Jesus over not being selected for a Nations League fixture in September.

That row led to the five-time Ballon d'Or winner being handed a 30-day suspension by the Portuguese football federation and his subsequent retirement, and Portugal looked liked they needed prime Ronaldo in a dismal display.

Denmark were the better team from the first minute and it was no surprise when the away team went in at the break thanks to Pierre-Emile's Hojbjerg's spot-kick in the 43rd minute.

Hojlund lashed home a brilliant volley four minutes after the restart and seven minutes later left the home crowd shellshocked with a strike of which Ronaldo would have been proud, the Napoli striker skipping past Ruben Dias before crashing home a powerful shot from the edge of the area.

Boos and whistles began to ring out at the Estadio Municipal when Joachim Andersen headed in the fourth, and Bernardo Silva's late consolation effort barely dampened the discontent.

Antonio Nusa opened the scoring for Norway just before the hour mark in Oslo with a fine low strike before Andreas Schjelderup tapped home a perfect low cross from Erling Haaland, who rounded off the scoring from the penalty spot two minutes from time.

Wales briefly had hope of getting a result when David Brook's chipped finish halved the deficit in the 79th minute, but Craig Bellamy's team will finish bottom of the group and risk being relegated to League B.

#2631. The NBA is all about fit. Paul George and the Celtics fit together perfectly.

We've all done it. We've judged players on various teams around the league based on preconceived notions and false narratives. We've jumped to conclusions based on what we think we know about someone rather than what we actually know.

That's human nature, and it's not our fault. When we see someone act a certain way once or twice, we assume they act that way all the time. Basketball mirrors life in the sense that one snapshot from the wrong point viewpoint can dictate how we feel about something or someone.

I'll be the first to admit that I was somewhat skeptical when the Celtics acquired Paul George. It wasn't that I didn't respect Paul George as a basketball player or respect him as a person. I did. It wasn't that I didn't think he could coexist next to Jayson Tatum. I did. It wasn't that I didn't think he still had anything left in the tank. I did.

But I didn't view George as a winner. I know he has made the Conference Finals three times, but I didn't view him as someone who could bring a team from very good to great and reach that next level.

I viewed him as someone who could put up stats with the best of them but those stats didn't carry much value, at least at this point in his career. I didn't think he could come in and seamlessly replace Jaylen Brown, play just as well if not better than Brown and help the Celtics maintain their perch as a true contender.

I was wrong. He can do all of those things, and he is proving it on a nightly basis. I feel like they should be printing out shirts that read: "I was wrong about Paul George" or "I owe Paul George an apology."

Every Seinfeld fan out there has enjoyed the Summer of George. Now, we get to enjoy the Season of George. He looks like a new man, both physically and spiritually, and someone who is playing with a freedom and purpose he hasn't had in years.

He resembles a player good enough to lead the Celtics to the promised land. I know, I know, it's only mid-November, and George has been injury prone throughout his career.

But, I think the Celtics have the right approach where he isn't playing every game. He looks fresh and is oozing with confidence and swagger right now. We all hope he avoids a major injury, but regardless of how it shakes out, let's make sure to enjoy what he's doing. George has gushed about the Celtics' culture, and it's clear the admiration is mutual as long as he keeps hooping.

George is the only player in the NBA averaging at least 22 points and 2 steals per game while shooting at least 44 percent from 3-point range. He has proven he is still a matchup nightmare who is fully capable of taking over a game.

That must have felt amazing to go into his old arena and light up the 76ers and take over a game that was pegged as a Tatum-Brown duel. On a court with those two stars, LeBron James, Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey, George was by far the best player.

I could describe George's style of play in many ways, but the one that comes to mind more than anything else is smooth. He has a fluidity to his game that I personally didn't fully appreciate watching him periodically on other teams. Part of it is health, obviously, but part of it is natural ability that was on full display in his Pacers days.

George is reminding everyone that he is still a dominant force. He might not be as explosive as he once was, but he is still crafty, versatile and highly skilled. He makes smart decisions, defends at a high level, doesn't force shots, fits in with the Celtics' system and is clutch.

I felt similarly about Kristaps Porzingis when he came to Boston. The Celtics have a knack for revitalizing injury-prone players who have (somewhat fairly and somewhat unfairly) developed a mixed reputation.

George is back to his old self, and as long as he keeps this up, the Celtics are very much in the mix to contend for an NBA title.

#2630. Chargers list Ladd McConkey as questionable for Monday night

Wide receiver Ladd McConkey is among the Chargers players listed as questionable for Monday night’s game against the Ravens.

McConkey is dealing with a back injury that kept him off of the practice field this week. McConkey has dealt with a number of injuries this season and has 17 catches for 286 yards and a touchdown on the season.

The Chargers also listed guard Kayode Awosika (fibula), defensive lineman Justin Eboigbe (ankle), tight end David Njoku (fibula), linebacker Denzel Perryman (pectoral), and offensive lineman Trey Pipkins (personal, knee) as questionable. Njoku, Perryman, and Pipkins will need to be activated from injured reserve in order to play against Baltimore.

Wide receiver Brenen Thompson (hamstring) is considered doubtful while guard Branson Taylor (ankle), cornerback Isas Waxter (ankle), and safety Kendall Williamson (calf) have been ruled out.
